Figure 6. Region Plots of Network Effects’ Influences on Platform’s Dominant Homing Policy From the Perspective on Social Welfare

Balancing profit maximization and social welfare is often a tough challenge for profitable organizations. However, an analysis of Figures 4 and 6 suggests exceptions in certain scenarios where the highest platform profits and maximum social welfare can be achieved together. Specifically, Figures 4b and 4c show that under the ???? policy, the platform can maximize its profits when network effects are pronounced. In a similar vein, Figures 6b and 6c indicate that the ???? policy is also effective in maximizing social welfare under strong network effects. This suggests that in cases where user participation modes are asymmetrical (ℱ?? or ??ℱ), the ???? policy can achieve both maximal platform profits and social welfare, but only with significant network effects. However, it’s important to note that this isn’t always possible. In situations where network effects are weaker, it becomes unfeasible for any policy to simultaneously optimize both platform profits and social welfare.

## 6 Conclusion

After a comprehensive analysis of various platform cases, we found three prevalent phenomena emerging from practice. First, platforms use exclusive partnerships (e.g., 24S’s sole luxury-brand listings on Louis Vuitton and Dior) or open multi-homing (e.g., drivers on Uber and Lyft) to shape user freedom. Second, platforms monetize choice through membership fees (e.g., Amazon Prime, Uber Pass) and invest in service enhancements—fast delivery, loyalty rewards, exclusive experiences—to gain an edge. Third, growth on one side (e.g., Didi’s drivers up 53.3% vs. passengers up 19.1%) yields an unequal value for the other, influencing optimal pricing and homing strategies. Building upon these observations, we constructed a stylized Hotelling model in a two-sided market consisting of duopoly platforms to comprehensively analyze critical business strategies, including platform pricing decisions, profits, user participation, user loyalty, and social welfare.

## 6.1 Main Findings

The analytical and numerical analyses reveal three main findings. First, we found that user freedom is not free. Freedom is an intangible, economically valuable asset. Platforms must strike a balance between the value of freedom and its cost. Our study suggests two distinct strategies based on homing policies: a pay-forfreedom strategy, where charges are applied when a platform adopts a multi-homing policy on both sides, and a freedom-premium strategy, which involves user subsidies on both sides in environments where the platform opts for a single-homing policy for one or both sides. Second, we discovered interesting spillover effects. We show that interside intraplatform spillovers—where one side’s service of the focal platform impacts the user loyalty to the other side—are positive under medium freedom but may be negative under complete freedom. Conversely, interside interplatform spillovers—where a rival platform’s service on one side affects user loyalty to the other side of the focal platform—are positive under complete freedom but negative under medium freedom. Third, we found an augmentation effect between platform service levels and user participation, which refers to circumstances in which an increase in the platform’s service level reliably attracts more users, regardless of the homing policy adopted by the platform.

## 6.2 Contributions and Implications

## 6.2.1 Theoretical Contributions

Our research makes several important contributions to information systems research. First, we bridge the literature on pricing and non-pricing differentiation, offering an integrated framework that examines both dimensions simultaneously. Prior studies often examine platform differentiation from a pricing or service level perspective in isolation. Our study is among the first to integrate both dimensions, offering a more complete framework for understanding platform strategy.

Second, we conceptualize freedom as an economic construct within platform markets, quantifying its value and analyzing its implications for platform strategy. We introduce the notion of user participation freedom as an economically valuable and strategically relevant construct. While previous literature discusses platform control or openness, few studies quantify the value of freedom or embed it within pricing strategies. Our model demonstrates how platforms must balance financial incentives against user freedom, enriching the theoretical understanding of strategic platform behavior.

Third, we uncover and characterize spillover dynamics across homing policies, highlighting how service levels on one side affect loyalty and participation on the other. Existing research has rarely explored how homing policies interact with cross-side and crossplatform effects. By distinguishing between interside intraplatform and interside interplatform spillovers under different homing configurations, our study deepens the literature on network effects, loyalty, and service provision.

## 6.2.2 Practical Implications

Our findings provide actionable insights for platform managers and policymakers. First, we provide guidance on the strategic use of homing policies, outlining how platforms can balance exclusivity and openness to optimize profits and participation. Platforms must carefully choose between single- and multi-homing strategies based on their competitive context and user-side dynamics. The right combination of pricing and subsidy strategies (e.g., pay-for-freedom vs. freedom-premium) can help platforms maintain a loyal and engaged user base.

Second, we offer insights into managing spillover effects, enabling platforms to create cross-side influences under different homing policies. Platforms should recognize that improving service on one side may not always yield direct benefits. Under certain homing conditions, such efforts can backfire, reducing loyalty either within the same platform or across competing platforms. Understanding these dynamics is critical for designing effective cross-side strategies.

Third, we address fairness and user expectations, offering guidance to platforms on managing the tradeoffs between user freedom, service quality, and loyalty. Our results indicate that switching from a single-single to a multi-multi homing policy gives users more freedom but reduces their access to premium services due to the platform’s profit goal. The tension between granting users more freedom and maintaining highquality service raises broader questions about fairness in digital markets. While greater freedom can benefit users, it may also reduce access to premium services if platforms pursue short-term profit maximization. Policymakers and regulators should examine how such strategic choices affect user welfare in the rapidly evolving digital economy. In a platform’s early stages— when rapid growth, user acquisition, and network effect building are paramount—the trade-off between user freedom and service provision may be less pronounced, as profitability is not yet the primary objective. Over time, however, as growth stabilizes and platforms shift toward monetization, profit-driven decisions make this trade-off increasingly salient, shaping both service design and long-term user relationship strategies.

## 6.3 Limitations and Future Research

It is worth noting that the homing policies (????, ????, ????) and service levels were treated as exogenous regimes in this study. While endogenizing homing decisions and service levels is an interesting direction, it is beyond the scope of the current model. Future research could delve into the dynamics and endogeneity of homing policies and service levels, assess the influence of non-price factors such as information provision on pricing strategies, and explore various user behaviors, such as switching in diverse market structures, including monopolistic and perfectly competitive markets.

Another limitation of our model lies in its assumption that platforms aim to maximize short-term profits. In reality, especially during early-stage development, many platforms prioritize long-term growth, user base expansion, and network effect accumulation—even at the expense of short-term profitability. For example, platforms like Uber and Pinduoduo have historically operated at a loss to aggressively acquire users and scale the ecosystem. While our results provide insights under the rational profit-maximization assumption, future work could extend the model to incorporate multi-stage or dynamic objectives, such as user growth or ecosystem development. This would yield richer implications for platform strategy in evolving competitive environments.

Then, the equilibrium prices on both sides are $\begin{array} { r } { p _ { i , j } ^ { S S * } = 2 t - \frac { \alpha _ { - i } ( 3 t + 2 \alpha _ { i } + \alpha _ { - i } + \theta _ { - i } \Delta s _ { - i , j } ^ { S S } ) } { 3 t } } \end{array}$ and $p _ { - i , j } ^ { S S * } = \frac { 1 } { 3 } ( 3 t - 4 \alpha _ { i } + \alpha _ { - i } + \theta _ { - i } \Delta s _ { - i , j } ^ { S S } )$ , then the platform’s optimal profit is $\begin{array} { r } { \pi _ { j } ^ { S S * } = \frac { 1 } { 1 8 t } \big [ 9 ( 4 t ^ { 2 } - \alpha _ { i } ^ { 2 } - 2 \alpha _ { i } \alpha _ { - i } - } \end{array}$ $\alpha _ { - i } ^ { 2 } ) + ( - 3 t + \alpha _ { i } + 2 \alpha _ { - i } - \theta _ { - i } \Delta s _ { - i , j } ^ { s s } ) ^ { 2 } \big ] - h _ { i } \big ( s _ { i , j } ^ { s s } \big ) ^ { 2 } - h _ { - i } \big ( s _ { - i , j } ^ { s s } \big ) ^ { 2 }$ . It is worth noting that $p _ { i j } ^ { S S * } = 2 ( t -$ $\alpha _ { - i } N _ { - i , j } ^ { S S * } )$ , which suggests that the price for partial participation is higher than that in the full participation scenario.

(3) Full participation on both sides (ℱℱ): if either platform provides significantly different service levels to users on both sides, i.e., $\begin{array} { r } { \Delta s _ { i , j } ^ { S S } \ge \frac { 3 t - ( 2 \alpha _ { i } + \alpha _ { - i } ) } { \theta _ { i } } . } \end{array}$ , all the users on both sides ?? and −?? will fully participate on platform ??, i.e., $N _ { i , j } ^ { S S * } = N _ { - i , j } ^ { S S * } = 1$ . Then, the equilibrium price is $p _ { i j } ^ { S S * } = 2 ( t - \alpha _ { - i } )$ , and the platform’s optimal profit is $\pi _ { j } ^ { S S * } =$ $4 t - 2 \alpha _ { i } - 2 \alpha _ { - i } - h _ { i } \big ( s _ { i , j } ^ { S S } \big ) ^ { 2 } - h _ { - i } \big ( s _ { - i , j } ^ { S S } \big ) ^ { 2 }$

Moreover, Figure A1 illustrates the impact of cross-side network effect and the difference in service levels provided between duopoly platforms on user participation mode. From the perspective of the service difference between the two platforms, if the service provided by the focal platform is lower than that provided by its competitors, i.e., $\Delta s _ { X , j } ^ { S S } < 0$ users will be more inclined to choose the platform with a higher service level, so only part of users may join the focal platform. In addition, the greater the service difference and the smaller the network effect, the lower the utility of users joining the focal platform, so the number of partial participations becomes smaller. On the contrary, if the focal platform has service advantages, i.e., $\Delta s _ { X , j } ^ { S S } > 0$ , the user participation mode also depends on the degree of network externalities. It shows that users prefer full participation (ℱℱ) with a greater network effect and a higher service difference; otherwise, they will choose partial participation (????). The reason is that when the focal platform has a stronger service advantage and larger network effect, users on side ?? will gain more net utility by joining the platform, which may induce a strong intention to participate. Thus, a significant number of users will participate. This observation is also consistent with the results of Lemma 1. Furthermore, according to Lemma 1, the impact of service level and network effect coefficient on the number of participating users and the service fee charged by the platform is summarized in Table A1.

Figure A1. Impact of Network Effect and Service Difference on User’s Participation (SS)

Table A1 shows that the number of users participating in the focal platform increases with the service level provided by the focal platform to them, and decreases with the service level provided by the competition platform, i.e., $\partial { \tilde { N } _ { i , j } ^ { S S * } } / \partial { s _ { i , j } ^ { S S } } \bar { \geq } 0$ and $\partial N _ { i , j } ^ { S S * } / \partial s _ { i , - j } ^ { S S } \leq 0$ always hold in all scenarios. This is understandable given that users tend to choose to join platforms with services. The better the services the focal platform provides, the more users will take part in this platform. Meanwhile, the better the services the competing platform offers, the more users will be attracted and leave the focal platform. The managerial implication is that in order to attract more users to join, the platform must make great efforts to outperform its competitors through a service advantage.

Furthermore, Figure A2 illustrates the impact of cross-side network effect and the difference in service level provided in duopoly on user participation mode. From the perspective of the service difference between the two platforms, if the service provided by the focal platform is lower than that provided by its competitors, i.e., $\Delta s _ { X , j } ^ { M M } < 0 ;$ all users will quit the focal platform and choose the competing platform which provides better services. This is because when the platform allows users to be multiple-homers, rational users will choose the platform with a higher service level to realize a higher utility. This is quite different from the observation when the platform provides a single-homing policy. Therefore, users will only join if the platform has a service advantage, i.e., $\Delta s _ { X , j } ^ { \mathbf { \hat { M } } M } > 0$ . Meanwhile, the user’s participation also depends on the network effect. It suggests that users prefer full participation (ℱℱ) with a greater network effect and a higher service difference; otherwise, they would choose partial participation (????). These finding are the same as the results under the ???? policy.

Both the price charged by the platform and the number of users who participated are increasing with the service level provided by the focal platform, i.e., $\partial p _ { i , j } ^ { M M * } \big / \partial s _ { i , j } ^ { M M } \geq 0$ and $\partial n _ { i , j } ^ { M M * } / \partial s _ { i , j } ^ { M \bar { M } } \geq 0$ in all scenarios from Table A2. It suggests that no matter what kind of participation mode the users adopt, the better services provided by the platform, the higher the service fee and the number of users.

## A.3 Additional Results of Lemma 3

According to Lemma 3, in the duopoly market the users on side $i ( i \in \{ X , Y \} )$ and side $- i ( - i \in \{ Y , X \} , - i \neq i )$ on platform $j \left( j \in \left\{ A , B \right\} \right)$ have the following participation modes.

(1) Partial participation on both sides (????): if the difference in the service level provided by either platform to users on the single-homing side (side ??) is moderate and the service sensitivity of the users on the multiple-homing side (side −?? ) is relatively high, i.e., ?????? $\begin{array} { r } { \left( - \frac { \left( C z _ { - j } + \alpha ^ { 2 } \theta _ { - i } \Delta s _ { - i } ^ { S M } \right) } { \alpha } , - ( C + \alpha \theta _ { - i } \Delta s _ { - i } ^ { S M } ) \right) < 2 t \theta _ { i } \Delta s _ { i , j } ^ { S M } < } \end{array}$ $\begin{array} { r } { m i n \left( \frac { C Z _ { j } - \alpha ^ { 2 } \theta _ { - i } \Delta s _ { - i } ^ { S M } } { \alpha } , C - \alpha \theta _ { - i } \Delta s _ { - i } ^ { S M } \right) } \end{array}$ ?????? $\theta _ { - i } > ( 2 t - \alpha ) / \big ( s _ { - i , j } ^ { S M } + s _ { - i , - j } ^ { S M } \big )$ , then the users on both sides will partially participate on platform ?? , i.e., $\begin{array} { r } { N _ { i , j } ^ { S M * } = \frac { C + H } { 2 C } < 1 } \end{array}$ and $\begin{array} { r } { N _ { - i , j } ^ { S M * } = \frac { ( \alpha _ { i } + \alpha _ { - i } ) H + C \left( \alpha _ { i } + \alpha _ { - i } + 2 s _ { - i , j } ^ { S M } \theta _ { - i } \right) } { 4 t C } < 1 } \end{array}$ , where $Z _ { j } = 4 t - 2 s _ { - i , j } ^ { S M } \theta _ { Y } - \alpha$ . Thus, the platform’s equilibrium price and corresponding profit are given in Lemma 3. (2) User partial participation on one side (ℱ?? or ??ℱ): It occurs when the service difference is significantly different, and the user’s sensitivity is medium. Specifically, when $\begin{array} { r } { \Delta s _ { i } ^ { S M } \ge \frac { C - \alpha \theta _ { - i } \Delta s _ { - i } ^ { S M } } { 2 t \theta _ { i } } } \end{array}$ , and $\frac { 2 t - \alpha } { s _ { - i , j } ^ { S M } + s _ { - i , - j } ^ { S M } } \le \theta _ { - i } <$ $\begin{array} { r } { m i n \left( \frac { 2 t } { s _ { - i , - j } ^ { S M } } , \frac { 2 t - \alpha } { s _ { - i , j } ^ { S M } } \right) } \end{array}$ , users will fully participate on the platform providing the single-homing policy and partially participate on the platform providing the multi-homing policy. Alternatively, if $\begin{array} { r } { \Delta s _ { i , j } ^ { S M } \ge \frac { C Z _ { j } - \alpha ^ { 2 } \theta _ { - i } \Delta s _ { - i } ^ { S M } } { 2 \alpha t \theta _ { i } } } \end{array}$ and $\frac { 2 t - \alpha } { s _ { - i , j } ^ { S M } } < \theta _ { Y } \leq$ ?????? $\left( \frac { 2 t } { s _ { - i , j } ^ { S M } } , \frac { 4 t - \alpha } { \left( s _ { - i , j } ^ { S M } + s _ { - i , - j } ^ { S M } \right) } \right)$ , users will partially participate on the platform providing the single-homing policy and fully participate on the platform providing the multi-homing policy. Then, we have $p _ { i , j } ^ { S M * } =$ $4 t ^ { 3 } - t \alpha _ { - i } ( 3 \alpha _ { i } + \alpha _ { - i } ) - \theta _ { - i } s _ { - i , j } ^ { S M } ( 2 t ^ { 2 } - \alpha _ { i } \alpha _ { - i } )$ ??(?? +?? ) $\begin{array} { r } { p _ { - i , j } ^ { S M * } = \frac { \theta _ { - i } s _ { Y , j } ^ { S M } \alpha _ { i } + t ( \alpha _ { - i } - \alpha _ { i } ) } { ( \alpha _ { i } + \alpha _ { - i } ) } } \end{array}$ $\begin{array} { r } { N _ { i , j } ^ { S M * } = \frac { 2 t - \theta _ { - i } s _ { - i , j } ^ { S M } } { \alpha _ { i } + \alpha _ { - i } } } \end{array}$ $n _ { - i , j } ^ { S M * } = 2 -$ $\begin{array} { r l } & { \frac { \alpha _ { i } + \alpha _ { - i } + \theta _ { Y } \left( s _ { - i , j } ^ { S M } + s _ { - i , j } ^ { S M } \right) } { 2 t } , \operatorname { a n d } \pi _ { j } ^ { S M * } = \frac { \theta _ { - i } ^ { 2 } s _ { - i , j } ^ { S M _ { 2 } } ( 2 t ^ { 2 } - \alpha _ { i } \alpha _ { - i } ) + t ( 8 t ^ { 2 } - \alpha _ { i } ^ { 2 } - 6 \alpha _ { i } \alpha _ { - i } - \alpha _ { - i } ^ { 2 } ) ( t - s _ { - i , j } ^ { S M } \theta _ { - i } ) } { t ( \alpha _ { i } + \alpha _ { - i } ) ^ { 2 } } - h _ { i } ( s _ { i , j } ^ { S M } ) ^ { 2 } - h _ { - i } ( s _ { - i , j } ^ { S M } ) ^ { 2 } . } \end{array}$

(3) Full participation on both sides (ℱℱ): if either platform provides significantly different service levels to users on both sides, i.e., $\Delta s _ { i , j } ^ { S M } \ge Q - W$ and $\theta _ { - i } = ( 2 t - \alpha ) / s _ { - i , j } ^ { S M }$ , all the users on both side ?? and side ?? will fully participate on platform ??, i.e., $N _ { i , j } ^ { S M * } = N _ { - i , j } ^ { S M * } = 1$ . Then, the equilibrium price is $\begin{array} { r } { p _ { i , j } ^ { S M * } = 2 t - \frac { \alpha _ { - i } \left( 3 \alpha _ { i } + \alpha _ { - i } + \theta _ { - i } s _ { - i , j } ^ { S M } \right) } { 2 t } } \end{array}$ 2 $\begin{array} { r } { p _ { - i , j } ^ { S M * } = \frac { \theta _ { - i } s _ { - i , j } ^ { S M } - \alpha _ { i } + \alpha _ { - i } } { 2 } } \end{array}$ , and the platform’s optimal profit is $\begin{array} { r } { \pi _ { j } ^ { S M * } = \frac { 4 t ^ { 2 } - \alpha _ { i } ( t + 3 \alpha _ { - i } ) + ( t - \alpha _ { - i } ) \left( \alpha _ { - i } + s _ { - i , j } ^ { S M } \theta _ { - i } \right) } { 2 t } } \end{array}$ $h _ { i } \big ( s _ { i , j } ^ { S M } \big ) ^ { 2 } - h _ { - i } \big ( s _ { - i , j } ^ { S M } \big ) ^ { 2 }$

As the platforms apply different homing policies for different users on two sides, Figure A3 respectively illustrates the impact of network effect and service differences on user participation. In Figure A3a, from the perspective of singlehomers (side X), the pattern is similar when platforms offered a single-homing policy. Namely, when the platform has a service disadvantage, fewer users will join; on the contrary, the stronger the service advantages of the platform, the higher the degree of user participation. But different from the scenario when platforms implement a single-homing policy, as a result of the platform providing asymmetric policy (that is, single-homing for one side and multi-homing for the other side), single-homers have limited choices when choosing which platform to join, so they will fully participate only when the focal platform has an extremely strong service advantage and the network effect is moderate. In addition, in Figure A3b, from the perspective of multi-homers (side Y), $\Delta s _ { Y , j } ^ { S M }$ equals to $s _ { Y , j } ^ { S M } - s _ { Y , - j } ^ { S M }$

Therefore, $\Delta s _ { Y , j } ^ { S M } > 0$ implies that the focal platform (i.e., platform ?? ) provides a higher service level than the competition platform $( \mathrm { i . e . , p l a t f o r m } - j )$ on side ??. In this case, customers on side ?? will go from partial participation to full participation with the increase of the positive gap between $s _ { Y , j } ^ { S M }$ and $s _ { Y , - j } ^ { S M }$ . It’s intuitive that better services will attract more customers. Something interesting is that when the focal platform offers a lower service level than the competition platform, i.e., $\Delta s _ { Y , j } ^ { S M } < 0$ , the customers on side ?? will also fully participate on the focal platform if $\alpha _ { Y }$ is relatively high. There are two plausible explanations. The first is that the cross-side network effect on side ?? is high, which means the customers on side ?? will incur more benefits than the customers on side ??. The second is related to pricing. Table A3. shows that $\partial p _ { - i , j } ^ { S M * } \big / \partial s _ { - i , j } ^ { S M } > 0$ in the cases of ℱℱ, ℱ??. This means that a higher service level on side ?? implies a higher price charged to side ??. Now $s _ { Y , j } ^ { S M }$ is lower than $s _ { Y , - j } ^ { S M }$ , which means the focal platform charges a lower service fee to side ?? than the competition platform. Therefore, although the focal platform provides a lower service level, it also incurs lower costs. As a result, the customers on side ?? may choose to fully participate on the focal platform. In addition, according to Lemma 3, the impact of service level and network effect coefficient on the number of users participating and the service fee charged by the platform is summarized in Table A3.

Table A3 reveals some interesting results. To illustrate, we use $i = X { \mathrm { ~ a n d } } - i = Y$ as an example. The rising $s _ { Y , j } ^ { S M }$ will decrease $p _ { X , j } ^ { S M * }$ but increase $p _ { Y , j } ^ { S M * }$ in the scenarios of $\mathcal { F F } , \mathcal { F P } , \mathcal { P F }$ . This means that when the focal provides a higher service to side ??, it will decrease the fee charged to side ?? and increase the fee charged to side ??. Recall that the platform allows single-homing on side ?? and multi-homing on side ??. A possible reason may be that when the focal platform offers a high service level to side ??, it will increase its costs. So, it should cover these costs through markups. However, the high price will drive away some users on side ??. In order to avoid or reduce leaving users on side ??, the focal platform could leverage the network effect. In other words, the focal platform may attract more users on side ?? through markdowns. Due to the network effect, the increased users on side ?? will attract more users on side ??.

Another noticeable result is that the cross-side externalities $\alpha _ { X }$ and $\alpha _ { Y }$ have the same impact on the price charged to side ?? , but have different impacts on the price charged to side ?? , i.e., $\partial p _ { X , j } ^ { S M * } / \partial \alpha _ { X } < 0 , \partial p _ { X , j } ^ { S M * } / \partial \alpha _ { Y } < 0$ but $\partial p _ { Y , j } ^ { S M * } / \partial \alpha _ { X } < 0 , \partial p _ { Y , j } ^ { S M * } / \partial \alpha _ { Y } > 0$

Recall that side X is single-homing, and side Y is multi-homing under the SM policy. With the increase of $\alpha _ { X } ,$ , the focal platform will decrease the price charged to side $Y ( \mathrm { i . e . , } \partial p _ { Y , j } ^ { S M * } / \partial \alpha _ { X } < 0 )$ because the focal platform can attract more users on side Y through markdowns and then incur more benefits to the users on side X due to greater network effects. Similarly, with the increase of $\alpha _ { Y }$ , the focal platform will decrease the price charged to side $X ( \mathrm { i } . \mathrm { e } . , \partial p _ { X , j } ^ { S M * } / \partial \alpha _ { Y } < 0 )$ Meanwhile, the fee charged to side X will also drop in $\alpha _ { X } ( \mathrm { i . e . , } \partial p _ { X , j } ^ { S M * } / \partial \alpha _ { X } < 0 )$ . The reason may be that with the increased network effect, side X becomes more attractive to the users. However, side ?? is single-homing, which means if users want to participate on side X, they can only choose one platform. So, the focal platform may use the markdown strategy to appeal to users.

Interestingly, the price charged to side Y by the focal platform will increase in the network effect of side $Y \ ( \mathrm { i . e . }$ $\partial p _ { Y , j } ^ { S M * } / \partial \bar { \alpha } _ { Y } > 0 )$ . The reason may be that the users on side Y are multi-homers. The focal platform should put in more effort to eventually draw the multi-homing user to consume on its platform, which will lead to more costs for the focal platform. Therefore, the focal platform would increase the price.

In conclusion, as long as the network effects increase (no matter $\alpha _ { X }$ or $\alpha _ { Y } ) _ { : }$ , the price charged to the single-homing side will decrease accordingly. However, the fee imposed on the side engaged in multi-homing will rise with the growth of its own network effect. Conversely, it will decline as the network effect on the other side increases.

According to Lemma 1-3, substituting the optimal platform profit $\pi _ { j } ^ { k * }$ and optimal user participation $N _ { i , j } ^ { k * } \left( i \in \left\{ X , Y \right\} \right.$ $j \in \{ A , B \}$ and $K \in \{ S S , S M , M M \} )$ into Equation (5), we have the optimal social warfare for each scenario as follows.

\begin{array}{r l} & S W ^ {S S *} = N _ {X, A} ^ {S S *} p _ {X, A} ^ {S S *} + N _ {X, B} ^ {S S *} p _ {X, B} ^ {S S *} + N _ {Y, A} ^ {S S *} p _ {Y, A} ^ {S S *} + N _ {Y, B} ^ {S S *} p _ {Y, B} ^ {S S *} - h _ {A} \big (s _ {X, A} ^ {S S} \big) ^ {2} - h _ {A} \big (s _ {Y, A} ^ {S S} \big) ^ {2} - h _ {B} \big (s _ {X, B} ^ {S S} \big) ^ {2} - h _ {B} \big (s _ {Y, B} ^ {S S} \big) ^ {2} \\ & \qquad + \int_ {0} ^ {N _ {X, A} ^ {S S *}} \big (V _ {i} + \alpha_ {X} N _ {Y, A} ^ {S S *} + \theta_ {X} s _ {X, A} ^ {S S} - p _ {X, A} ^ {S S *} - t x \big) d x \\ & \qquad + \int_ {N _ {X, A} ^ {S S *}} ^ {1} \big (V + \alpha_ {X} N _ {Y, B} ^ {S S *} + \theta_ {X} s _ {X, B} ^ {S S} - p _ {X, B} ^ {S S *} - t (1 - x) \big) d x + \int_ {0} ^ {N _ {Y, A} ^ {S S *}} \big (V + \alpha_ {Y} N _ {X, A} ^ {S S *} + \theta_ {Y} s _ {Y, A} ^ {S S} - p _ {Y, A} ^ {S S *} - t y \big) d y \\ & \qquad + \int_ {N _ {Y, A} ^ {S S *}} ^ {1} \big (V + \alpha_ {Y} N _ {X, B} ^ {S S *} + \theta_ {Y} s _ {Y, B} ^ {S S} - p _ {Y, B} ^ {S S *} - t (1 - y) \big) d y. \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} S W ^ {S M *} = N _ {X, A} ^ {S M *} p _ {X, A} ^ {S M *} + N _ {X, B} ^ {S M *} p _ {X, B} ^ {S M *} + N _ {Y, A} ^ {S M *} p _ {Y, A} ^ {S M *} + N _ {Y, B} ^ {S M *} p _ {Y, B} ^ {S M *} - h _ {A} \big (s _ {X, A} ^ {S M} \big) ^ {2} - h _ {A} \big (s _ {Y, A} ^ {S M} \big) ^ {2} - h _ {B} \big (s _ {X, B} ^ {S M} \big) ^ {2} - h _ {B} \big (s _ {Y, B} ^ {S M} \big) ^ {2} \\ \qquad + \int_ {0} ^ {N _ {X, A} ^ {S M *}} \big (V + \alpha_ {X} N _ {Y, A} ^ {S M *} + \theta_ {X} s _ {X, A} ^ {S M} - p _ {X, A} ^ {S M *} - t x \big) d x \\ \qquad + \int_ {N _ {X, A} ^ {S M *}} ^ {1} \big (V + \alpha_ {X} N _ {Y, B} ^ {S M *} + \theta_ {X} s _ {X, B} ^ {S M} - p _ {X, B} ^ {S M *} - t (1 - x) \big) d x + \int_ {0} ^ {n _ {Y, A} ^ {S M *}} \big (V + \alpha_ {Y} N _ {X, A} ^ {S M *} + \theta_ {Y} s _ {Y, A} ^ {S M} - p _ {Y, A} ^ {S M *} - t y \big) d y \\ \qquad + \int_ {0} ^ {n _ {Y, B} ^ {S M *}} \big (V + \alpha_ {Y} N _ {X, B} ^ {S M *} + \theta_ {Y} s _ {Y, B} ^ {S M} - p _ {Y, B} ^ {S M *} - t z \big) d z \\ \qquad + \int_ {0} ^ {N _ {Y, B} ^ {S M *} - n _ {Y, B} ^ {S M *}} \big (V + \alpha_ {Y} + \theta_ {Y} s _ {Y, A} ^ {S M} + \theta_ {Y} s _ {Y, B} ^ {S M} - p _ {Y, A} ^ {S M *} - p _ {Y, B} ^ {S M *} - t \big) d z. \end{array}

For side X, the indifference point of choosing platform ?? (where ?? = ??, ??) by setting $U _ { X , A } ^ { S S } ( \hat { x } _ { X } ^ { S S } ) =$ $U _ { X , B } ^ { S S } ( \hat { x } _ { X } ^ { S S } )$ gives the indifference value is $\begin{array} { r } { \hat { x } _ { X } ^ { S S } = \frac { t - p _ { X , A } ^ { S S } + p _ { X , B } ^ { S S } + \theta _ { X } s _ { X , A } ^ { S S } - \theta _ { X } s _ { X , B } ^ { S S } + \alpha _ { X } N _ { Y , A } ^ { S S } - \alpha _ { X } N _ { Y , B } ^ { S S } } { 2 t } } \end{array}$ . Similarly, we have the indifference point for side Y of choosing between platforms A and B as $\begin{array} { r } { \hat { x } _ { Y } ^ { S S } = \frac { t - p _ { Y , A } ^ { S S } + p _ { Y , B } ^ { S S } + \theta _ { Y } s _ { Y , A } ^ { S S } - \theta _ { Y } s _ { Y , B } ^ { S S } + \alpha _ { Y } N _ { Y , A } ^ { S S } - \alpha _ { Y } N _ { X , B } ^ { S S } } { \gamma } } \end{array}$ 2?? Then, substituting $N _ { X , A } ^ { S S } = \hat { x } _ { X } ^ { S S } , N _ { X , B } ^ { S S } = 1 - \hat { x } _ { X } ^ { S S } , N _ { Y , A } ^ { S S } = \hat { x } _ { Y } ^ { S S }$ , and $N _ { Y , B } ^ { S S } = 1 - \hat { x } _ { Y } ^ { S S }$ , we have

$$

$$

Substituting $N _ { X , A } ^ { S S } , N _ { Y , A } ^ { S S } , N _ { X , B } ^ { S S }$ and ??<sub>??,??</sub><sup>????</sup> to Equation (3), it follows,

$$
\begin{array}{r} \pi_ {A} ^ {S S} = - h _ {X} (s _ {X, A} ^ {S S}) ^ {2} - h _ {Y} (s _ {Y, A} ^ {S S}) ^ {2} + \frac {t p _ {X , A} ^ {S S} [ t + \theta_ {X} (s _ {X , A} ^ {S S} - s _ {X , B} ^ {S S}) - (p _ {X , A} ^ {S S} - p _ {X , B} ^ {S S}) ] + \alpha_ {X} [ p _ {Y , B} ^ {S S} - p _ {Y , A} ^ {S S} - \alpha_ {Y} + \theta_ {Y} (s _ {Y , A} ^ {S S} - s _ {Y , B} ^ {S S}) ]}{2 (t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y})} \\ + \frac {t p _ {Y , A} ^ {S S} [ t + \theta_ {Y} (s _ {Y , A} ^ {S S} - s _ {Y , B} ^ {S S}) - (p _ {Y , A} ^ {S S} - p _ {Y , B} ^ {S S}) ] + \alpha_ {Y} [ p _ {X , B} ^ {S S} - p _ {X , A} ^ {S S} - \alpha_ {X} + \theta_ {X} (s _ {X , A} ^ {S S} - s _ {X , B} ^ {S S}) ]}{2 (t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y})}, \end{array}
$$

\begin{array}{r} \pi_ {B} ^ {S S} = - h _ {X} (s _ {X, B} ^ {S S}) ^ {2} - h _ {Y} (s _ {Y, B} ^ {S S}) ^ {2} + \frac {t p _ {X , B} ^ {S S} [ t + \theta_ {X} (s _ {X , B} ^ {S S} - s _ {X , A} ^ {S S}) - (p _ {X , B} ^ {S S} - p _ {X , A} ^ {S S}) ] + \alpha_ {X} [ p _ {Y , A} ^ {S S} - p _ {Y , B} ^ {S S} - \alpha_ {Y} + \theta_ {Y} (s _ {Y , B} ^ {S S} - s _ {Y , A} ^ {S S}) ]}{2 (t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y})} \\ + \frac {t p _ {Y , B} ^ {S S} [ t + \theta_ {Y} (s _ {Y , B} ^ {S S} - s _ {Y , A} ^ {S S}) - (p _ {Y , B} ^ {S S} - p _ {Y , A} ^ {S S}) ] + \alpha_ {Y} [ p _ {X , A} ^ {S S} - p _ {X , B} ^ {S S} - \alpha_ {X} + \theta_ {X} (s _ {X , B} ^ {S S} - s _ {X , A} ^ {S S}) ]}{2 (t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y})}. \end{array}
$$

Then, we formulate the constrained optimization problem using Lagrangian function as

$$
\begin{array}{r l} & L _ {A} ^ {S S} \big (p _ {X, A} ^ {S S}, p _ {Y, A} ^ {S S} \big) = \pi_ {A} ^ {S S} \big (p _ {X, A} ^ {S S}, p _ {Y, A} ^ {S S} \big) + \beta_ {1} N _ {X, A} ^ {S S} + \beta_ {3} N _ {Y, A} ^ {S S} \\ & \qquad = \pi_ {A} ^ {S S} \big (p _ {X, A} ^ {S S}, p _ {Y, A} ^ {S S} \big) + \beta_ {1} \Bigg (\frac {t \big [ t + \theta_ {X} \big (s _ {X , A} ^ {S S} - s _ {X , B} ^ {S S} \big) - \big (p _ {X , A} ^ {S S} - p _ {X , B} ^ {S S} \big) \big ] + \alpha_ {X} \big [ p _ {Y , B} ^ {S S} - p _ {Y , A} ^ {S S} - \alpha_ {Y} + \theta_ {Y} \big (s _ {Y , A} ^ {S S} - s _ {Y , B} ^ {S S} \big) \big ]}{2 (t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y})} \Bigg) \\ & \qquad + \beta_ {3} \Bigg (\frac {t \big [ t + \theta_ {Y} \big (s _ {Y , A} ^ {S S} - s _ {Y , B} ^ {S S} \big) - \big (p _ {Y , A} ^ {S S} - p _ {Y , B} ^ {S S} \big) \big ] + \alpha_ {Y} \big [ p _ {X , B} ^ {S S} - p _ {X , A} ^ {S S} - \alpha_ {X} + \theta_ {X} \big (s _ {X , A} ^ {S S} - s _ {X , B} ^ {S S} \big) \big ]}{2 (t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y})} \Bigg), \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{r l} & L _ {B} ^ {S S} \big (p _ {X, B} ^ {S S}, p _ {Y, B} ^ {S S} \big) = \pi_ {B} ^ {S S} \big (p _ {X, B} ^ {S S}, p _ {Y, B} ^ {S S} \big) + \beta_ {2} N _ {X, B} ^ {S S} + \beta_ {4} N _ {Y, B} ^ {S S} \\ & \qquad = \pi_ {B} ^ {S S} \big (p _ {X, B} ^ {S S}, p _ {Y, B} ^ {S S} \big) + \beta_ {2} \left(\frac {t \big [ t + \theta_ {X} (s _ {X , B} ^ {S S} - s _ {X , A} ^ {S S}) - (p _ {X , B} ^ {S S} - p _ {X , A} ^ {S S}) \big ] + \alpha_ {X} \big [ p _ {Y , A} ^ {S S} - p _ {Y , B} ^ {S S} - \alpha_ {Y} + \theta_ {Y} (s _ {Y , B} ^ {S S} - s _ {Y , A} ^ {S S}) \big ]}{2 (t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y})}\right) \\ & \qquad \qquad + \beta_ {4} \left(\frac {t \big [ t + \theta_ {Y} (s _ {Y , B} ^ {S S} - s _ {Y , A} ^ {S S}) - (p _ {Y , B} ^ {S S} - p _ {Y , A} ^ {S S}) \big ] + \alpha_ {Y} \big [ p _ {X , A} ^ {S S} - p _ {X , B} ^ {S S} - \alpha_ {X} + \theta_ {X} (s _ {X , B} ^ {S S} - s _ {X , A} ^ {S S}) \big ]}{2 (t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y})}\right). \end{array}
$$

Then, the corresponding Karush–Kuhn–Tucker (KKT) conditions are

$$
\frac {\partial L _ {A} ^ {S S}}{\partial p _ {X , A} ^ {S S}} = \frac {\alpha_ {X} (p _ {Y , B} ^ {S S} - p _ {Y , A} ^ {S S} + \theta_ {Y} (s _ {Y , A} ^ {S S} - s _ {Y , B} ^ {S S})) - \alpha_ {Y} (p _ {Y , A} ^ {S S} + \beta_ {3} + \alpha_ {X})}{2 (t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y})} + \frac {t (- 2 p _ {X , A} ^ {S S} + p _ {X , B} ^ {S S} + \theta_ {X} (s _ {X , A} ^ {S S} - s _ {X , B} ^ {S S}) - \beta_ {1} + t)}{2 (t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y})} = 0,
$$

$$
\frac {\partial L _ {A} ^ {S S}}{\partial p _ {Y , A} ^ {S S}} = \frac {\alpha_ {Y} (p _ {X , B} ^ {S S} - p _ {X , A} ^ {S S} + \theta_ {X} (s _ {X , A} ^ {S S} - s _ {X , B} ^ {S S})) - \alpha_ {X} (p _ {X , A} ^ {S S} + \beta_ {1} + \alpha_ {Y})}{2 (t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y})} + \frac {t (- 2 p _ {Y , A} ^ {S S} + p _ {Y , B} ^ {S S} + \theta_ {Y} (s _ {Y , A} ^ {S S} - s _ {Y , B} ^ {S S}) - \beta_ {3} + t)}{2 (t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y})} = 0,
$$

$$
\frac {\partial L _ {B} ^ {S S}}{\partial p _ {X , B} ^ {S S}} = \frac {\alpha_ {X} (p _ {Y , A} ^ {S S} - p _ {Y , B} ^ {S S} + \theta_ {Y} (s _ {Y , B} ^ {S S} - s _ {Y , A} ^ {S S})) - \alpha_ {Y} (p _ {Y , B} ^ {S S} + \beta_ {4} + \alpha_ {X})}{2 (t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y})} + \frac {t (- 2 p _ {X , B} ^ {S S} + p _ {X , A} ^ {S S} + \theta_ {X} (s _ {X , B} ^ {S S} - s _ {X , A} ^ {S S}) - \beta_ {2} + t)}{2 (t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y})} = 0,

According to Equation (1) and (2), we have $U _ { X , A } ^ { M M } = V _ { X } + \alpha _ { X } N _ { Y , A } ^ { M M } + \theta _ { X } s _ { X , A } ^ { M M } - p _ { X , A } ^ { M M } - t \hat { x } _ { X } , U _ { X , B } ^ { M M } = V _ { X } + \alpha _ { X } N _ { Y , B } ^ { M M } +$ $\theta _ { X } s _ { X , B } ^ { M M } - p _ { X , B } ^ { M M } - t \check { x } _ { X }$ and $U _ { X , A B } ^ { M M } = V _ { X } + \alpha _ { X } \big ( N _ { Y , A } ^ { M M } + N _ { Y , B } ^ { M M } \big ) + \theta _ { X } s _ { X , A } ^ { M M } + \theta _ { X } s _ { X , B } ^ { M M } - p _ { X , A } ^ { M M } - p _ { X , B } ^ { M M } - t _ { X , A } ^ { M M }$ . (where $j = A , B )$ by setting $U _ { X , A } ^ { M M } ( \hat { x } _ { X } ) = U _ { X , A B } ^ { M M }$ gives the indifference value of is $\begin{array} { r } { \widehat { x } _ { X } \ = \frac { t + p _ { X , B } ^ { M M } - \theta _ { X } s _ { X , B } ^ { M M } - \alpha _ { X } N _ { Y , B } ^ { M M } } { t } } \end{array}$ . Then taking the second-order partial derivative with respect to $p _ { X , A } ^ { M M } , p _ { X , B } ^ { M M } , p _ { Y , A } ^ { M M }$ and $p _ { Y , B } ^ { M M }$ , we have

\begin{array}{r} \frac {\partial^ {2} L _ {A} ^ {M M}}{\partial (p _ {X , A} ^ {M M}) ^ {2}} = \frac {\partial^ {2} L _ {B} ^ {M M}}{\partial (p _ {X , B} ^ {M M}) ^ {2}} = \frac {\partial^ {2} L _ {A} ^ {M M}}{\partial (p _ {Y , A} ^ {M M}) ^ {2}} = \frac {\partial^ {2} L _ {B} ^ {M M}}{\partial (p _ {Y , B} ^ {M M}) ^ {2}} = - \frac {2 t}{t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y}}, \\ \frac {\partial^ {2} L _ {A} ^ {M M}}{\partial p _ {X , A} ^ {M M} \partial p _ {Y , A} ^ {M M}} = \frac {\partial^ {2} L _ {B} ^ {M M}}{\partial p _ {X , B} ^ {M M} \partial p _ {Y , B} ^ {M M}} = 0. \end{array}

and the Hessian matrix

According to Equation (1) and (2), we have $U _ { X , A } ^ { S M } = V _ { X } + \alpha _ { X } N _ { Y , A } ^ { S M } + \theta _ { X } s _ { X , A } ^ { S M } - p _ { X , A } ^ { S M } - t x _ { X }$ and $U _ { X , B } ^ { S M } = V _ { X } + \alpha _ { X } N _ { Y , B } ^ { S M } +$ $\theta _ { X } s _ { X , B } ^ { S M } - p _ { X , B } ^ { S M } - t ( 1 - x _ { X } )$ . Similarly, for side $Y , \mathrm { w e }$ have $U _ { Y , A } ^ { S M } = V _ { Y } + \alpha _ { Y } N _ { X , A } ^ { S M } + \theta _ { Y } S _ { Y , A } ^ { S M } - p _ { Y , A } ^ { S M } - t \hat { y } _ { Y } ^ { S M } , U _ { Y , B } ^ { S M } = V _ { Y } + \alpha _ { Y } N _ { X , B } ^ { S M } + \theta _ { Y } S _ { Y , B } ^ { S M } - p _ { Y , B } ^ { S M } -$ $t \check { y } _ { Y } ^ { S M }$ and $U _ { Y , A B } ^ { S M } = V _ { Y } + \alpha _ { Y } \big ( N _ { X , A } ^ { S M } + N _ { X , B } ^ { S M } \big ) + \theta _ { Y } s _ { Y , A } ^ { S M } + \theta _ { Y } s _ { Y , B } ^ { S M } - p _ { Y , A } ^ { S M } - p _ { Y , B } ^ { S M } - t _ { Y , A } ^ { S M } ,$ For side ?? , one indifference point of choosing platform ?? (where $j = A , B \ )$ by setting $U _ { Y , A } ^ { S M } ( \hat { y } _ { Y } ^ { S M } ) = U _ { Y , A B } ^ { S M }$ gives the indifference value is $\hat { y } _ { Y } ^ { S M } =$ $\frac { t + p _ { Y , B } ^ { S M } - \theta _ { Y } s _ { Y , B } ^ { S M } - \alpha _ { Y } N _ { X , B } ^ { S M } } { t } .$ . Then, we formulate the constrained optimization problem using Lagrangian function as

$$

$$
\begin{array}{r l} & L _ {B} ^ {S M} \big (p _ {X, B} ^ {S M}, p _ {Y, B} ^ {S M} \big) = \pi_ {B} ^ {S M} \big (p _ {X, B} ^ {S M}, p _ {Y, B} ^ {S M} \big) + \beta_ {2} N _ {X, B} ^ {S M} + \beta_ {5} n _ {Y, B} ^ {S M} \\ & \qquad = \pi_ {B} ^ {S M} \big (p _ {X, B} ^ {S M}, p _ {Y, B} ^ {S M} \big) + \beta_ {2} \left(\frac {t \big [ t + \theta_ {X} (s _ {X , B} ^ {S M} - s _ {X , A} ^ {S M}) - (p _ {X , B} ^ {S M} - p _ {X , A} ^ {S M}) \big ] + \alpha_ {X} \big [ p _ {Y , A} ^ {S M} - p _ {Y , B} ^ {S M} - \alpha_ {Y} + \theta_ {Y} (s _ {Y , B} ^ {S M} - s _ {Y , A} ^ {S M}) \big ]}{2 (t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y})}\right) \\ & \qquad + \beta_ {5} \left(\frac {t (t + p _ {Y , A} ^ {S M})}{(t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y})} - \frac {\theta_ {Y} s _ {Y , A} ^ {S M})}{t}\right) \\ & \qquad - \frac {\alpha_ {Y} \big (t (t + p _ {X , B} ^ {S M} - p _ {X , A} ^ {S M} - \theta_ {X} (s _ {X , B} ^ {S M} - s _ {X , A} ^ {S M})) + \alpha_ {X} (p _ {Y , B} ^ {S M} + p _ {Y , A} ^ {S M} - \alpha_ {Y} - \theta_ {Y} (s _ {Y , B} ^ {S M} - s _ {Y , A} ^ {S M}) + 2 t) \big)}{2 t (t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y})}. \end{array}
$$

|&{\beta_4}\left( {\frac{t(t+ p_{Y,B}^{\textit{SM}})}{(t^2-α_Xα_Y)}} - {\frac{\theta_Ys_{Y,B}^{\textit{SM}}}{t}} - {\frac{\alpha_Y\big(t(t+ p_{X,A}^{\textit{SM}}-p_{X,B}^{\textit{SM}}-θ_X(s_{X,A}^{\textit{SM}}-s_{X,B}^{\textit{SM}}))+α_X(p_{Y,A}^{\textit{SM}}+p_{Y,B}^{\textit{SM}}-α_Y-θ_Y(s_{Y,A}^{\textit{SM}}-s_{Y,B}^{\textit{SM}})+2t)\big)}{2t(t^2-α_Xα_Y)}}\right) = 0,\\&{\beta_5}\left( {\frac{t(t+ p_{Y,A}^{\textit{SM}})}{(t^2-α_Xα_Y)}} - {\frac{\theta_Ys_{Y,A}^{\textit{SM}}}{t}} - {\frac{\alpha_Y\big(t(t+ p_{X,B}^{\textit{SM}}-p_{X,A}^{\textit{SM}}-θ_X(s_{X,B}^{\textit{SM}}-s_{X,A}^{\textit{SM}}))+α_X(p_{Y,B}^{\textit{SM}}+p_{Y,A}^{\textit{SM}}-α_Y-θ_Y(s_{Y,B}^{\textit{SM}}-s_{Y,A}^{\textit{SM}})+2t)\big)}{2t(t^2-α_Xα_Y)}}\right) = 0,\\&{\beta_i\geq 0,i=1,2,3,4,5.}\end{array}

Then, we have the following feasible cases

(1) $\mathrm { I f } \beta _ { 1 } = 0 , \beta _ { 2 } = 0 , \beta _ { 3 } = 0 , \beta _ { 4 } = 0 \mathrm { a n d } \beta _ { 5 } = 0 \mathrm { : }$

In this case, we have all the constraint conditions are non-binding, i.e., $0 \leq N _ { X , A } ^ { S M * } < 1 , 0 \leq N _ { X , B } ^ { S M * } < 1 , n _ { Y , A } ^ { S M * } \geq$ $0 , n _ { Y , B } ^ { S M * } \geq 0$ , and $n _ { Y , A } ^ { S M * } + n _ { Y , B } ^ { S M * } < 1$ . Then, taking the second-order partial derivative with respect to $p _ { X , A } ^ { S M } , ~ p _ { X , B } ^ { S M } ,$ $p _ { Y , A } ^ { S M }$ and $p _ { Y , B } ^ { S M }$ , we have

$$
\begin{array}{r} \frac {\partial^ {2} L _ {A} ^ {S M}}{\partial (p _ {X , A} ^ {S M}) ^ {2}} = \frac {\partial^ {2} L _ {B} ^ {S M}}{\partial (p _ {X , B} ^ {S M}) ^ {2}} = \frac {t}{- t ^ {2} + \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y}}, \\ \frac {\partial^ {2} L _ {A} ^ {S M}}{\partial (p _ {Y , A} ^ {S M}) ^ {2}} = \frac {\partial^ {2} L _ {B} ^ {S M}}{\partial (p _ {Y , B} ^ {S M}) ^ {2}} = \frac {- 2 t ^ {2} + \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y}}{t (t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y})}, \\ \frac {\partial^ {2} L _ {A} ^ {S M}}{\partial p _ {X , A} ^ {S M} \partial p _ {Y , A} ^ {S M}} = \frac {\partial^ {2} L _ {B} ^ {S M}}{\partial p _ {X , B} ^ {S M} \partial p _ {Y , B} ^ {S M}} = - \frac {\alpha_ {X} + \alpha_ {Y}}{2 t ^ {2} - 2 \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y}}. \end{array}
$$

and the Hessian matrix

$$
H = \left[ \begin{array}{c c} \frac {t}{- t ^ {2} + \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y}} & - \frac {\alpha_ {X} + \alpha_ {Y}}{2 t ^ {2} - 2 \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y}} \\ - \frac {\alpha_ {X} + \alpha_ {Y}}{2 t ^ {2} - 2 \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y}} & \frac {- 2 t ^ {2} + \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y}}{t (t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y})} \end{array} \right].
$$

When $t > \sqrt { \frac { \alpha _ { X } ^ { 2 } + 6 \alpha _ { X } \alpha _ { Y } + \alpha _ { Y } ^ { 2 } } { 8 } }$ , the first-order leading principal minor $\begin{array} { r } { H _ { 1 } = \frac { t } { - t ^ { 2 } + \alpha _ { X } \alpha _ { Y } } < 0 } \end{array}$ and the second-order leading principal minor $\begin{array} { r } { H _ { 2 } = \frac { ( 8 t ^ { 2 } - \alpha _ { X } ^ { 2 } - 6 \alpha _ { X } \alpha _ { Y } - \alpha _ { Y } ^ { 2 } ) } { 4 ( t ^ { 2 } - \alpha _ { X } \alpha _ { Y } ) ^ { 2 } } > 0 } \end{array}$ , i.e., the Hessian matrix is negative definite. Then

$$
\partial L _ {A} ^ {S M} / \partial p _ {X, A} ^ {S M} = 0, \partial L _ {A} ^ {S M} / \partial p _ {Y, A} ^ {S M} = 0, \partial L _ {B} ^ {S M} / \partial p _ {X, B} ^ {S M} = 0 \mathrm{and} \partial L _ {B} ^ {S M} / \partial p _ {Y, B} ^ {S M} = 0 \mathrm{give}
$$

$$
p _ {X, A} ^ {S M *} = - \frac {s _ {Y , A} ^ {S M} \alpha_ {Y} \theta_ {Y}}{2 t} + \frac {4 t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {Y} (3 \alpha_ {X} + \alpha_ {Y})}{4 t} + \frac {(4 t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {Y} (3 \alpha_ {X} + \alpha_ {Y})) (2 t \theta_ {X} (s _ {X , A} ^ {S M} - s _ {X , B} ^ {S M}) + \theta_ {Y} (s _ {Y , A} ^ {S M} - s _ {Y , B} ^ {S M}) (\alpha_ {X} + \alpha_ {Y}))}{4 t (6 t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} ^ {2} - 4 \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y} - \alpha_ {Y} ^ {2})},
$$

$$
p _ {X, B} ^ {S M *} = - \frac {s _ {Y , B} ^ {S M} \alpha_ {Y} \theta_ {Y}}{2 t} + \frac {4 t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {Y} (3 \alpha_ {X} + \alpha_ {Y})}{4 t} + \frac {(4 t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {Y} (3 \alpha_ {X} + \alpha_ {Y})) (2 t \theta_ {X} (s _ {X , B} ^ {S M} - s _ {X , A} ^ {S M}) + \theta_ {Y} (s _ {Y , B} ^ {S M} - s _ {Y , A} ^ {S M}) (\alpha_ {X} + \alpha_ {Y}))}{4 t (6 t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} ^ {2} - 4 \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y} - \alpha_ {Y} ^ {2})},
$$

$$
p _ {Y, A} ^ {S M *} = \frac {(\alpha_ {X} - \alpha_ {Y}) (2 t \theta_ {X} (s _ {X , A} ^ {S M} - s _ {X , B} ^ {S M}) + \theta_ {Y} (s _ {Y , A} ^ {S M} - s _ {Y , B} ^ {S M}) (\alpha_ {X} + \alpha_ {Y}))}{- 4 (6 t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} ^ {2} - 4 \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y} - \alpha_ {Y} ^ {2})} - \frac {(\alpha_ {X} - \alpha_ {Y} - 2 \theta_ {Y} s _ {Y , A} ^ {S M})}{4},
$$

$$
p _ {Y, B} ^ {S M *} = \frac {(\alpha_ {X} - \alpha_ {Y}) (2 t \theta_ {X} (s _ {X , B} ^ {S M} - s _ {X , A} ^ {S M}) + \theta_ {Y} (s _ {Y , B} ^ {S M} - s _ {Y , A} ^ {S M}) (\alpha_ {X} + \alpha_ {Y}))}{- 4 (6 t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} ^ {2} - 4 \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y} - \alpha_ {Y} ^ {2})} - \frac {(\alpha_ {X} - \alpha_ {Y} - 2 \theta_ {Y} s _ {Y , B} ^ {S M})}{4}.
$$

By substituting $p _ { X , A } ^ { S M * } , p _ { X , B } ^ { S M * } , p _ { Y , A } ^ { S M * }$ and $p _ { Y , B } ^ { S M * }$ into Equations (B.11)−(B.14),

$$
N _ {X, A} ^ {S M *} = \frac {1}{2} + \frac {t \theta_ {X} (s _ {X , A} ^ {S M} - s _ {X , B} ^ {S M}) + \theta_ {Y} (s _ {Y , A} ^ {S M} - s _ {Y , B} ^ {S M}) (\alpha_ {X} + \alpha_ {Y})}{(6 t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} ^ {2} - 4 \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y} - \alpha_ {Y} ^ {2})},
$$

$$
N _ {X, B} ^ {S M *} = \frac {1}{2} + \frac {t \theta_ {X} (s _ {X , B} ^ {S M} - s _ {X , A} ^ {S M}) + \theta_ {Y} (s _ {Y , B} ^ {S M} - s _ {Y , A} ^ {S M}) (\alpha_ {X} + \alpha_ {Y})}{(6 t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} ^ {2} - 4 \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y} - \alpha_ {Y} ^ {2})},
$$

$$
N _ {Y, A} ^ {S M *} = \frac {(\alpha_ {X} + \alpha_ {Y}) (2 t \theta_ {X} (s _ {X , A} ^ {S M} - s _ {X , B} ^ {S M}) + \theta_ {Y} (s _ {Y , A} ^ {S M} - s _ {Y , B} ^ {S M}) (\alpha_ {X} + \alpha_ {Y}))}{4 t (6 t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} ^ {2} - 4 \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y} - \alpha_ {Y} ^ {2})} + \frac {(\alpha_ {X} + \alpha_ {Y} + 2 s _ {Y , A} ^ {S M} \theta_ {Y})}{4 t},
$$

$$
N _ {Y, B} ^ {S M *} = \frac {(\alpha_ {X} + \alpha_ {Y}) (2 t \theta_ {X} (s _ {X , B} ^ {S M} - s _ {X , A} ^ {S M}) + \theta_ {Y} (s _ {Y , B} ^ {S M} - s _ {Y , A} ^ {S M}) (\alpha_ {X} + \alpha_ {Y}))}{4 t (6 t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} ^ {2} - 4 \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y} - \alpha_ {Y} ^ {2})} + \frac {(\alpha_ {X} + \alpha_ {Y} + 2 s _ {Y , B} ^ {S M} \theta_ {Y})}{4 t},
$$

Therefore, the optimal platform profit is

$$
\begin{array}{r} \pi_ {A} ^ {S M *} = - h _ {X} (s _ {X, A} ^ {S M}) ^ {2} - h _ {Y} (s _ {Y, A} ^ {S M}) ^ {2} + \frac {\theta_ {Y} ^ {2} (s _ {Y , A} ^ {S M}) ^ {2}}{4 t} + \frac {8 t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} ^ {2} - 6 \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y} - \alpha_ {Y} ^ {2}}{1 6 t (6 t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {x} ^ {2} - 4 \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y} - \alpha_ {y} ^ {2})} \\ + \frac {(8 t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} ^ {2} - 6 \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y} - \alpha_ {Y} ^ {2}) (2 t \theta_ {X} (s _ {X , A} ^ {S M} - s _ {X , B} ^ {S M}) + \theta_ {Y} (s _ {Y , A} ^ {S M} - s _ {Y , B} ^ {S M}) (\alpha_ {X} + \alpha_ {Y})) ^ {2}}{1 6 t (6 t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} ^ {2} - 4 \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y} - \alpha_ {Y} ^ {2}) ^ {2}}, \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{r} \pi_ {B} ^ {S M *} = - h _ {X} (s _ {X, B} ^ {S M}) ^ {2} - h _ {Y} (s _ {Y, B} ^ {S M}) ^ {2} + \frac {\theta_ {Y} ^ {2} (s _ {Y , B} ^ {S M}) ^ {2}}{4 t} + \frac {8 t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} ^ {2} - 6 \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y} - \alpha_ {Y} ^ {2}}{1 6 t (6 t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {x} ^ {2} - 4 \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y} - \alpha_ {y} ^ {2})} \\ + \frac {(8 t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} ^ {2} - 6 \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y} - \alpha_ {Y} ^ {2}) (2 t \theta_ {X} (s _ {X , B} ^ {S M} - s _ {X , A} ^ {S M}) + \theta_ {Y} (s _ {Y , B} ^ {S M} - s _ {Y , A} ^ {S M}) (\alpha_ {X} + \alpha_ {Y})) ^ {2}}{1 6 t (6 t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} ^ {2} - 4 \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y} - \alpha_ {Y} ^ {2}) ^ {2}}. \end{array}
$$

(2) If $\beta _ { 1 } = 0 , \beta _ { 2 } > 0 , \beta _ { 3 } > 0 , \beta _ { 4 } = 0$ and $\begin{array} { r } { \beta _ { 5 } > 0 , \mathrm { ~ i . e . , } \alpha _ { X } ^ { 2 } + 6 \alpha _ { X } \alpha _ { Y } + \alpha _ { Y } ^ { 2 } < 8 t ^ { 2 } \leq \frac { 4 } { 2 } \big ( 2 t \theta _ { X } ( s _ { X , A } ^ { S M } - s _ { X , B } ^ { S M } ) + \left( 2 t - s _ { X , A } ^ { S M } \right) - 8 t ^ { 2 } \big ) , } \end{array}$ $\theta _ { Y } s _ { Y , B } ^ { S M } \big ) ( \alpha _ { X } + \alpha _ { Y } ) + \alpha _ { X } \alpha _ { Y } \big )$ and $- 2 t + \theta _ { Y } s _ { Y , A } ^ { S M } + \alpha _ { X } + \alpha _ { Y } = 0$ . In this case, we have $N _ { X , A } ^ { S M * } = 1$ , ?????? $N _ { Y , A } ^ { S M * } = 1$ Then we have $\begin{array} { r } { p _ { X , A } ^ { S M * } = 2 t - \frac { \alpha _ { Y } ( t + \alpha _ { X } ) } { t } , p _ { Y , A } ^ { S M * } = - t + \theta _ { Y } s _ { Y , A } ^ { S M } + \alpha _ { Y } } \end{array}$ . Therefore, substituting $N _ { X , A } ^ { S M * } , \ N _ { Y , A } ^ { S M * } , p _ { X , A } ^ { S M * }$ and $p _ { Y , A } ^ { S M * }$ into Equation (3), the optimal platform profit is $\begin{array} { r } { \pi _ { A } ^ { S M * } = \frac { 4 t ^ { 2 } - \alpha _ { X } ( t + 3 \alpha _ { Y } ) + ( t - \alpha _ { Y } ) ( \alpha _ { Y } + s _ { Y , A } ^ { S M } \theta _ { Y } ) } { 2 t } - h _ { X } \left( s _ { X , A } ^ { S M } \right) ^ { 2 } - h _ { Y } \left( s _ { Y , A } ^ { S M } \right) ^ { 2 } } \end{array}$

(3) (4) If $\beta _ { 1 } > 0 , \beta _ { 2 } = 0 , \beta _ { 3 } > 0 , \beta _ { 4 } > 0$ and $\begin{array} { r } { \beta _ { 5 } = 0 , \mathrm { ~ i . e . , ~ } \alpha _ { X } ^ { 2 } + 6 \alpha _ { X } \alpha _ { Y } + \alpha _ { Y } ^ { 2 } < 8 t ^ { 2 } \leq \frac { 4 } { 3 } \big ( 2 t \theta _ { X } ( s _ { X , B } ^ { S M } - s _ { X , A } ^ { S M } ) + } \end{array}$ $\big ( 2 t - \theta _ { Y } s _ { Y , A } ^ { S M } \big ) ( \alpha _ { X } + \alpha _ { Y } ) + \alpha _ { X } \alpha _ { Y } \big )$ and $- 2 t + \theta _ { Y } s _ { Y , B } ^ { S M } + \alpha _ { X } + \alpha _ { Y } = 0$ . Given the same service level, $\mathrm { i . e . , } s _ { i , j } ^ { S S } = s _ { i , j } ^ { M M }$ and $s _ { - i , j } ^ { S S } =$ $s _ { - i , j } ^ { M M }$ , we have $\begin{array} { r } { \pi _ { j } ^ { S S * } - \pi _ { j } ^ { M M * } = \frac { 2 } { t } ( t ^ { 2 } + \alpha _ { i } \alpha _ { - i } - ( \alpha _ { i } + \alpha _ { - i } ) t ) } \end{array}$ . Since $t > ( \alpha _ { i } + \alpha _ { - i } ) / 2$ and $t ^ { 2 } > \alpha _ { i } \alpha _ { - i } ,$ , we have $\pi _ { j } ^ { S S * } - \pi _ { j } ^ { M M * } > 0$ . Therefore, we have $\pi _ { j } ^ { S S * } > \pi _ { j } ^ { M M * }$ when $N _ { i , j } ^ { k * } = N _ { - i , j } ^ { k * } = 1$

b. For case $\mathcal { F P }$ and ??ℱ, from Lemma 1 and 2, we have

$$
\pi_ {j} ^ {S S *} = \frac {1}{1 8 t} \big [ 9 (4 t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {i} ^ {2} - 2 \alpha_ {i} \alpha_ {- i} - \alpha_ {- i} ^ {2}) + (- 3 t + \alpha_ {i} + 2 \alpha_ {- i} - \theta_ {- i} \Delta s _ {- i, j} ^ {S S}) ^ {2} \big ] - h _ {i} \big (s _ {i, j} ^ {S S} \big) ^ {2} - h _ {- i} \big (s _ {- i, j} ^ {S S} \big) ^ {2},
$$

$$
\pi_ {j} ^ {S M *} = \frac {\theta_ {- i} ^ {2} s _ {- i , j} ^ {S M 2} (2 t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {i} \alpha_ {- i}) + t (8 t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {i} ^ {2} - 6 \alpha_ {i} \alpha_ {- i} - \alpha_ {- i} ^ {2}) (t - s _ {- i , j} ^ {S M} \theta_ {- i})}{t (\alpha_ {i} + \alpha_ {- i}) ^ {2}} - h _ {i} \big (s _ {i, j} ^ {S M} \big) ^ {2} - h _ {- i} \big (s _ {- i, j} ^ {S M} \big) ^ {2},
$$

and

$$
\pi_ {j} ^ {M M *} = \frac {(t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {i} \alpha_ {- i})}{t} + \frac {\left[ - t \alpha_ {- i} (\alpha_ {i} + s _ {i , - j} ^ {M M} \theta_ {i}) + (2 t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {i} \alpha_ {- i}) (\alpha_ {- i} + s _ {- i , j} ^ {M M} \theta_ {- i}) \right] ^ {2}}{(t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {i} \alpha_ {- i}) (4 t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {i} \alpha_ {- i}) ^ {2}} - h _ {X} (s _ {i, j} ^ {M M}) ^ {2} - h _ {Y} (s _ {- i, j} ^ {M M}) ^ {2}.

Given the same service level, i.e., $s _ { i , j } ^ { S S } = s _ { i , j } ^ { S M } = s _ { i , j } ^ { M M } = s _ { i , j }$ and $s _ { - i , j } ^ { S S } = s _ { - i , j } ^ { S M } = s _ { - i , j } ^ { M M } = s _ { - i , j } .$ , we have the following cases to discuss.

$$

where $Z = ( \alpha _ { i } + s _ { i , j } \theta _ { j } ) ( - 2 t ^ { 2 } + \alpha _ { i } \alpha _ { - i } ) + t \alpha _ { i } ( \alpha _ { - i } + s _ { - i , - j } \theta _ { - i } ) .$

In addition, from the proof of Lemma 2, we have $\begin{array} { r } { ( 2 t ^ { 2 } - \alpha _ { i } \alpha _ { - i } ) \big [ - ( 2 t ^ { 2 } - \alpha _ { i } \alpha _ { - i } ) + t \big ( \alpha _ { i } + s _ { i , j } \theta _ { i } \big ) \big ] \geq t ^ { 2 } \alpha _ { i } s _ { - i , - j } \theta _ { - i } , } \end{array}$ and $\begin{array} { r } { ( t ^ { 2 } - \alpha _ { i } \alpha _ { - i } ) ( 4 t ^ { 2 } - \alpha _ { i } \alpha _ { - i } ) \big [ 2 t ^ { 2 } - \alpha _ { i } \alpha _ { - i } - t \big ( s _ { i , j } ^ { M M } \theta _ { i } + \alpha _ { i } \big ) \big ] < - t \alpha _ { i } \big \{ ( 2 t ^ { 2 } - \alpha _ { i } \alpha _ { - i } ) \big [ ( 2 t ^ { 2 } - \alpha _ { i } \alpha _ { - i } ) - t \big ( \alpha _ { - i } + \alpha _ { - i } \alpha _ { j } \big ) \big ] - \alpha _ { i } \alpha _ { - i } \big \} } \end{array}$ $s _ { - i , j } \theta _ { - i } \big ) \big ] + t ^ { 2 } \alpha _ { - i } s _ { i , - j } \theta _ { i } \big \}$ . ii. According to platform profit with MM and SM, we have

$$
\begin{array}{r l} & {\frac {\partial \big (\pi_ {j} ^ {M M *} - \pi_ {j} ^ {S M *} \big)}{\partial \alpha_ {- i}} = \frac {\alpha_ {i} (s _ {- i , j} ^ {2} \theta_ {- i} ^ {2} - (\alpha_ {i} + \alpha_ {- i}) ^ {2}) + 2 t (3 \alpha_ {i} + \alpha_ {- i}) (t - s _ {- i , j} \theta_ {- i})}{t (\alpha_ {i} + \alpha_ {- i}) ^ {2}}} \\ & {\qquad + \frac {2 (s _ {- i , j} ^ {2} \theta_ {- i} ^ {2} (2 t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {i} \alpha_ {- i}) + t (8 t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {i} ^ {2} - 6 \alpha_ {i} \alpha_ {- i} - \alpha_ {- i} ^ {2}) (t - s _ {- i , j} \theta_ {- i}))}{t (\alpha_ {i} + \alpha_ {- i}) ^ {3}}} \\ & {\qquad + \frac {2 Z \alpha_ {i} (t + \alpha_ {i} + s _ {i , j} \theta_ {i})}{(t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {i} \alpha_ {- i}) (4 t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {i} \alpha_ {- i}) ^ {2}} + \frac {3 Z ^ {2} \alpha_ {i} (2 t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {i} \alpha_ {- i})}{(t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {i} \alpha_ {- i}) ^ {2} (4 t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {i} \alpha_ {- i}) ^ {3}}.} \end{array}
$$

According to the proof of Lemma 3, we have $8 t ^ { 2 } - \alpha _ { i } ^ { 2 } - 6 \alpha _ { i } \alpha _ { - i } - \alpha _ { - i } ^ { 2 } > 0$ and $2 t - \alpha _ { i } - \alpha _ { - i } \le s _ { - i , j } \theta _ { - i } \le 2 t$ Therefore, we have $\partial \big ( \pi _ { j } ^ { M M * } - \pi _ { j } ^ { S M * } \big ) / \partial \alpha _ { - i } \geq 0$ holds

## B.6 Proof of Proposition 3

When users fully participate, i.e., $N _ { i j } ^ { k * } = 1$ . Therefore, here, we only discuss the scenario where users participate partially, i.e., $N _ { i j } ^ { k * } < 1$

(1) In the scenario of single-homing policy $( \mathrm { i . e . , } k = S S )$ , according to Lemma 1 and taking the partial derivative of $N _ { i , j } ^ { S S * }$ with regard to $s _ { i , j } ^ { S S * }$ yields

$$
\frac {\partial N _ {X , A} ^ {S S *}}{\partial s _ {X , A} ^ {S S}} = \frac {\partial N _ {X , B} ^ {S S *}}{\partial s _ {X , B} ^ {S S}} = \frac {\partial N _ {Y , A} ^ {S S *}}{\partial s _ {Y , A} ^ {S S}} = \frac {\partial N _ {Y , B} ^ {S S *}}{\partial s _ {Y , B} ^ {S S}} = \frac {3 t \theta_ {X}}{2 [ 9 t ^ {2} - (2 \alpha_ {X} + \alpha_ {Y}) (2 \alpha_ {Y} + \alpha_ {X}) ]}.
$$

The numerator $\partial N _ { i , j } ^ { S S * } / \partial s _ { i , j } ^ { S S }$ is obviously positive, and the denominator equals $9 t ^ { 2 } - 2 ( \alpha _ { X } + \alpha _ { Y } ) ^ { 2 } - \alpha _ { X } \alpha _ { Y }$ . Since $2 t > \alpha _ { X } + \alpha _ { Y }$ and $t ^ { 2 } > \alpha _ { X } \alpha _ { Y }$ , we have $9 t ^ { 2 } - ( 2 \alpha _ { X } + \alpha _ { Y } ) ( 2 \alpha _ { Y } + \alpha _ { X } ) > 0$ . So, $\partial N _ { i , j } ^ { S S * } / \partial s _ { i , j } ^ { S S } > 0$ hold.

(2) In the scenario of multi-homing policy $( \mathrm { i . e . , } k = M M )$ , according to Lemma 2 and taking the partial derivative of $N _ { i , j } ^ { M M * }$ with regard to $s _ { i , j } ^ { M M * }$ yields

$$
\frac {\partial N _ {X , A} ^ {M M *}}{\partial s _ {X , A} ^ {S S}} = \frac {\partial N _ {X , B} ^ {M M *}}{\partial s _ {X , B} ^ {S S}} = \frac {\partial N _ {Y , A} ^ {M M *}}{\partial s _ {Y , A} ^ {S S}} = \frac {\partial N _ {Y , B} ^ {M M *}}{\partial s _ {Y , B} ^ {S S}} = \frac {t \theta_ {X} (2 t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y})}{(t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y}) (4 t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y})}.
$$

Since $t ^ { 2 } > \alpha _ { X } \alpha _ { Y }$ , both the numerator and the denominator of $\partial N _ { i , j } ^ { M M * } / \partial s _ { i , j } ^ { M M }$ are positive, we have $\partial N _ { i , j } ^ { M M * } / \partial s _ { i , j } ^ { M M } > 0$

(3) In the scenario of single-multi-homing policy $( \mathrm { i } . \mathsf { e } . , k = S M )$ , according to Lemma 3 and taking the partial derivative of $N _ { i , j } ^ { S M * }$ with regard to $s _ { i , j } ^ { S M * }$ yields

$$
\frac {\partial N _ {X , A} ^ {S M *}}{\partial s _ {X , A} ^ {S S}} = \frac {\partial N _ {X , B} ^ {S M *}}{\partial s _ {X , B} ^ {S S}} = \frac {t \theta_ {X}}{(6 t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} ^ {2} - 4 \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y} - \alpha_ {Y} ^ {2})},
$$

$$
\frac {\partial N _ {Y , A} ^ {S M *}}{\partial s _ {Y , A} ^ {S S}} = \frac {\partial N _ {Y , B} ^ {S M *}}{\partial s _ {Y , B} ^ {S S}} = \frac {\theta_ {Y} (\alpha_ {X} + \alpha_ {Y}) ^ {2}}{4 t (6 t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} ^ {2} - 4 \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y} - \alpha_ {Y} ^ {2})} + \frac {\theta_ {Y}}{2 t},
$$

It is straightforward to find that the numerator $\partial N _ { i , j } ^ { S M * } / \partial s _ { i , j } ^ { S M }$ is obviously. In addition, since $2 t > \alpha _ { X } + \alpha _ { Y }$ and $t ^ { 2 } >$ $\alpha _ { X } \alpha _ { Y }$ , we have $6 t ^ { 2 } - \alpha _ { X } ^ { 2 } - 4 \alpha _ { X } \alpha _ { Y } - \alpha _ { Y } ^ { 2 } = 6 t ^ { 2 } - ( \alpha _ { X } + \alpha _ { Y } ) ^ { 2 } - 2 \alpha _ { X } \alpha _ { Y } > 0$ . Therefore, $\partial N _ { i , j } ^ { S S * } / \partial s _ { i , j } ^ { S S } > 0$ hold. In summary, $\partial N _ { i , j } ^ { k * } / \partial s _ { i , j } ^ { k } > 0$ always holds.

\frac {\partial L _ {X , A} ^ {M M *}}{\partial s _ {Y , A} ^ {M M}} = - \frac {\theta_ {Y}}{\alpha_ {Y}} <   0.

ii. For case $\mathcal { P F } _ { \mathbf { \Phi } }$ , we have

$$
\frac {\partial n _ {X , A} ^ {M M *}}{\partial s _ {Y , A} ^ {M M}} = 0, a n d \frac {\partial N _ {X , A} ^ {M M *}}{\partial s _ {Y , A} ^ {M M}} = \frac {t \theta_ {X} (2 t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y})}{(t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y}) (4 t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y})} > 0.
$$

$\mathrm { S o , }$ we have

$$
\frac {\partial L _ {X , A} ^ {M M *}}{\partial s _ {Y , A} ^ {M M}} = - \frac {t \theta_ {Y} (t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y}) (4 t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y})}{t ^ {2} \alpha_ {Y} (- t \alpha_ {X} (\alpha_ {Y} + s _ {Y B} ^ {M M} \theta_ {Y}) + (2 t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y}) (\alpha_ {X} + s _ {X A} ^ {M M} \theta_ {X}))} <   0.
$$

iii. For case $\mathcal { F P }$ ?????? ${ \mathcal { P P } } _ { }$ , we have

$$
\frac {\partial n _ {X , A} ^ {M M *}}{\partial s _ {Y , A} ^ {M M}} = \frac {t ^ {2} \alpha_ {X} \theta_ {Y}}{(t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y}) (4 t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y})} > 0, a n d \frac {\partial N _ {X , A} ^ {M M *}}{\partial s _ {Y , A} ^ {M M}} = 0.
$$

So, we have

$$
\frac {\partial L _ {X , A} ^ {M M *}}{\partial s _ {Y , A} ^ {M M}} = \frac {t ^ {2} \alpha_ {X} \theta_ {Y}}{(t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y}) (4 t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y})} > 0.
$$

Therefore, we have $\partial L _ { X , A } ^ { M M * } / \partial s _ { Y , A } ^ { M M } \geq 0$ for case $\mathcal { F P }$ ?????? ????, and $\partial L _ { X , A } ^ { M M * } / \partial s _ { Y , A } ^ { M M } \leq 0$ for cases $\mathcal { F F }$ and $\mathcal { P F }$

d. Interside interplatform spillover effect

i. For case $\mathcal { F F }$ ?????? ${ \mathcal { F P } } ,$ we have $\partial n _ { X , A } ^ { M M * } \big / \partial s _ { Y , B } ^ { M M } = 0 _ { i }$ , ?????? $\partial N _ { X , A } ^ { M M * } \big / \partial s _ { Y , B } ^ { M M } = 0 . \mathrm { S o }$ , we have $\partial L _ { X , A } ^ { M M * } \big / \partial s _ { Y , B } ^ { M M } = 0$

ii. For case ??ℱ, we have

$$
\frac {\partial n _ {X , A} ^ {M M *}}{\partial s _ {Y , B} ^ {M M}} = 0, a n d \frac {\partial N _ {X , A} ^ {M M *}}{\partial s _ {Y , B} ^ {M M}} = - \frac {t ^ {2} \alpha_ {X} \theta_ {Y}}{(t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y}) (4 t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y})} <   0.
$$

So, we have

$$
\frac {\partial L _ {X , A} ^ {M M *}}{\partial s _ {Y , B} ^ {M M}} = \frac {\alpha_ {X} \theta_ {Y} (t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y}) (4 t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y}) (2 t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y} - t s _ {Y , A} ^ {M M} \theta_ {Y})}{t \alpha_ {Y} ((- t \alpha_ {X} (\alpha_ {Y} + s _ {Y , B} ^ {M M} \theta_ {Y}) + (2 t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y}) (\alpha_ {X} + s _ {X , A} ^ {M M} \theta_ {X})) ^ {2}} > 0.
$$

iii. For case ????, we have

$$
\frac {\partial n _ {X , A} ^ {M M *}}{\partial s _ {Y , B} ^ {M M}} = 0, a n d \frac {\partial N _ {X , A} ^ {M M *}}{\partial s _ {Y , B} ^ {M M}} = - \frac {t ^ {2} \alpha_ {X} \theta_ {Y}}{(t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y}) (4 t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y})} <   0.
$$

So, we have

$$
\frac {\partial L _ {X , A} ^ {M M *}}{\partial s _ {Y , B} ^ {M M}} = \frac {\alpha_ {X} \theta_ {Y} \big ((2 t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y}) (- \alpha_ {X} (t + \alpha_ {Y}) + t (2 t - s _ {X , B} ^ {M M} \theta_ {X})) + t ^ {2} s _ {Y , A} ^ {M M} \alpha_ {X} \theta_ {Y} \big)}{\big ((- t \alpha_ {X} (\alpha_ {Y} + s _ {Y , B} ^ {M M} \theta_ {Y}) + (2 t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y}) (\alpha_ {X} + s _ {X , A} ^ {M M} \theta_ {X})) \big) ^ {2}} > 0.
$$

Therefore, $\partial L _ { X , A } ^ { M M * } \big / \partial s _ { Y , B } ^ { M M } \geq 0$ always holds.

## B.8 Proof of Proposition 5

According to Lemma 3, we have the following cases to discuss.

a. self-influence

i. For case ℱℱ, we have $\partial n _ { Y , A } ^ { S M * } \big / \partial s _ { Y , A } ^ { S M } = 0 ,$ , and $\partial N _ { Y , A } ^ { S M * } \big / \partial s _ { Y , A } ^ { S M } = 0$ . So, we have $\partial L _ { Y , A } ^ { S M * } \big / \partial s _ { Y , A } ^ { S M } = 0$

ii. For case ℱ??, we have $\partial n _ { Y , A } ^ { S M * } \big / \partial s _ { Y , A } ^ { S M } = 0$ , and

$$
\frac {\partial N _ {Y , A} ^ {S M *}}{\partial s _ {Y , A} ^ {S M}} = \frac {\theta_ {Y}}{2 t} > 0.
$$

So, we have

$$
\frac {\partial N _ {Y , A} ^ {S M *}}{\partial s _ {Y , A} ^ {S M}} = \frac {\theta_ {Y} (- 2 t + \theta_ {Y} s _ {Y , B} ^ {S M})}{(\theta_ {Y} s _ {Y , A} ^ {S M} + \alpha_ {X} + \alpha_ {Y}) ^ {2}} <   0.
$$

iii. For case ??ℱ, we have $\partial N _ { Y , A } ^ { S M * } \big / \partial s _ { Y , A } ^ { S M } = 0 .$ , and

$$
\frac {\partial n _ {Y , A} ^ {S M *}}{\partial s _ {Y , A} ^ {S M}} = - \frac {\theta_ {Y}}{2 t} <   0.
$$

So, we have

$$
\frac {\partial L _ {Y , A} ^ {S M *}}{\partial s _ {Y , A} ^ {S M}} = - \frac {\theta_ {Y}}{2 t} <   0.
$$

iv. For case ????, we have

$$
\frac {\partial n _ {Y , A} ^ {S M *}}{\partial s _ {Y , A} ^ {S M}} = \frac {\theta_ {Y} (\alpha_ {X} + \alpha_ {Y}) ^ {2}}{4 t (6 t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} ^ {2} - 4 \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y} - \alpha_ {Y} ^ {2})} > 0,
$$

and

$$
\frac {\partial N _ {Y , A} ^ {S M *}}{\partial s _ {Y , A} ^ {S M}} = \frac {\theta_ {Y} (1 2 t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} ^ {2} - 6 \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y} - \alpha_ {Y} ^ {2})}{4 t (6 t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} ^ {2} - 4 \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y} - \alpha_ {Y} ^ {2})} > 0.
$$

So, we have

$$
\frac {\partial L _ {Y , A} ^ {S M *}}{\partial s _ {Y , A} ^ {S M}} = \frac {\theta_ {X} (\alpha_ {X} + \alpha_ {Y})}{2 (6 t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} ^ {2} - 4 \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y} - \alpha_ {Y} ^ {2})} > 0.
$$

Therefore, $\partial L _ { Y , A } ^ { S M * } / \partial s _ { Y , A } ^ { S M } \leq 0$ always holds.

b. Intraside cross-platform spillover effect

i. For cases ℱℱ, ℱ?? and ??ℱ, we have $\partial N _ { Y , A } ^ { S M * } \big / \partial s _ { Y , B } ^ { S M } = 0 _ { \scriptscriptstyle { \it Y , A } }$ and

$$
\frac {\partial n _ {Y , A} ^ {S M *}}{\partial s _ {Y , B} ^ {S M}} = - \frac {\theta_ {Y}}{2 t} <   0.
$$

So, we have

$$
\frac {\partial L _ {Y , A} ^ {S M *}}{\partial s _ {Y , B} ^ {S M}} = - \frac {\theta_ {Y}}{2 t} <   0.
$$

ii. For case ????, we have

$$
\frac {\partial n _ {Y , A} ^ {S M *}}{\partial s _ {Y , B} ^ {S M}} = - \frac {\theta_ {Y} (1 2 t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} ^ {2} - 6 \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y} - \alpha_ {Y} ^ {2})}{4 t (6 t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} ^ {2} - 4 \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y} - \alpha_ {Y} ^ {2})} <   0, \frac {\partial N _ {Y , A} ^ {S M *}}{\partial s _ {Y , B} ^ {S M}} = - \frac {\theta_ {Y} (\alpha_ {X} + \alpha_ {Y}) ^ {2}}{4 t (6 t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} ^ {2} - 4 \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y} - \alpha_ {Y} ^ {2})} <   0, a n d
$$

$$
\frac {\partial N _ {Y , A} ^ {S M *}}{\partial s _ {Y , B} ^ {S M}} <   \frac {\partial n _ {Y , A} ^ {S M *}}{\partial s _ {Y , B} ^ {S M}} <   0.
$$

So, we have

$$
\frac {\partial L _ {Y , A} ^ {S M *}}{\partial s _ {Y , B} ^ {S M}} = - \frac {4 \theta_ {Y} C \left(2 s _ {Y , A} ^ {S M} \theta_ {Y} (3 t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y}) - t (\alpha_ {X} + \alpha_ {Y}) (- 3 t + \theta_ {X} (- s _ {X , A} ^ {S M} + s _ {X , B} ^ {S M}) + \alpha_ {X}) + \alpha_ {Y} (t + \alpha_ {X})\right)}{((\alpha_ {X} + \alpha_ {Y}) H + C (\alpha_ {X} + \alpha_ {Y} + 2 s _ {Y , A} ^ {S M} \theta_ {Y})) ^ {2}} <   0.
$$

Therefore, $\partial L _ { Y , A } ^ { S M * } / \partial s _ { Y , B } ^ { S M } \leq 0$ always holds.

c. Interside interplatform spillover effect

i. For cases ℱℱ , ℱ?? and $\mathcal { P F }$ , we have $\partial n _ { Y , A } ^ { S M * } \big / \partial s _ { X , A } ^ { S M } = 0 .$ , and $\partial N _ { Y , A } ^ { S M * } \big / \partial s _ { X , A } ^ { S M } = 0$ . So, we have $\partial L _ { Y , A } ^ { S M * } \big / \partial s _ { X , A } ^ { S M } = 0 .$

ii. For case ????, we have

$$
\frac {\partial n _ {Y , A} ^ {S M *}}{\partial s _ {X , A} ^ {S M}} = \frac {\theta_ {X} (\alpha_ {X} + \alpha_ {Y})}{2 (6 t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} ^ {2} - 4 \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y} - \alpha_ {Y} ^ {2})} > 0, a n d \frac {\partial N _ {Y A} ^ {S M *}}{\partial s _ {X A} ^ {S M}} = \frac {\theta_ {X} (\alpha_ {X} + \alpha_ {Y})}{2 (6 t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} ^ {2} - 4 \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y} - \alpha_ {Y} ^ {2})} > 0,
$$

So, we have

$$
\frac {\partial L _ {Y , A} ^ {S M *}}{\partial s _ {X , A} ^ {S M}} = \frac {4 t \theta_ {X} C (\alpha_ {X} + \alpha_ {Y}) (- 2 t + \alpha_ {X} + \alpha_ {Y} + s _ {Y , A} ^ {S M} \theta_ {Y} + s _ {Y , B} ^ {S M} \theta_ {Y})}{((\alpha_ {X} + \alpha_ {Y}) H + C (\alpha_ {X} + \alpha_ {Y} + 2 s _ {Y , A} ^ {S M} \theta_ {Y})) ^ {2}} > 0.
$$

Therefore, $\partial L _ { Y , A } ^ { S M * } / \partial s _ { X , A } ^ { S M } \geq 0$ always holds.

d. Interside cross-platform spillover effect

i. For cases $\mathcal { F F } , \mathcal { F P } _ { \mathrm { : } }$ , and ??ℱ, we have $\partial n _ { Y , A } ^ { S M * } \big / \partial s _ { X , B } ^ { S M } = 0 , \mathrm { a n d } \partial N _ { Y , A } ^ { S M * } \big / \partial s _ { X , B } ^ { S M } = 0 . \mathrm { S o } ,$ , we have $\partial L _ { Y , A } ^ { S M * } \big / \partial s _ { X , B } ^ { S M } = 0$

ii. For case ????, we have

$$
\frac {\partial n _ {Y , A} ^ {S M *}}{\partial s _ {X , B} ^ {S M}} = - \frac {\theta_ {X} (\alpha_ {X} + \alpha_ {Y})}{2 (6 t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} ^ {2} - 4 \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y} - \alpha_ {Y} ^ {2})} <   0, a n d \frac {\partial N _ {Y , A} ^ {S M *}}{\partial s _ {X , B} ^ {S M}} = - \frac {\theta_ {X} (\alpha_ {X} + \alpha_ {Y})}{2 (6 t ^ {2} - \alpha_ {X} ^ {2} - 4 \alpha_ {X} \alpha_ {Y} - \alpha_ {Y} ^ {2})} <   1, b.
$$

So, we have

$$
\frac {\partial L _ {Y , A} ^ {S M *}}{\partial s _ {X , B} ^ {S M}} = - \frac {4 t \theta_ {X} C (\alpha_ {X} + \alpha_ {Y}) (- 2 t + \alpha_ {X} + \alpha_ {Y} + s _ {Y , A} ^ {S M} \theta_ {Y} + s _ {Y , B} ^ {S M} \theta_ {Y})}{((\alpha_ {X} + \alpha_ {Y}) H + C (\alpha_ {X} + \alpha_ {Y} + 2 s _ {Y , A} ^ {S M} \theta_ {Y})) ^ {2}} <   0.
$$

Therefore, $\partial L _ { Y , A } ^ { S M * } / \partial s _ { X , B } ^ { S M } \leq 0$ always holds.

## About the Authors

Jizhou Lu received his PhD degrees from Xi’an Jiaotong University and City University of Hong Kong. He is currently an associate professor in the Department of Supply Chain and Operations Management at Central University of Finance and Economics. His research interests include supply chain management, operations strategy, information sharing, and decision-making under uncertainty, with a focus on both theoretical modeling and practical applications in manufacturing and retail contexts. His work has appeared in European Journal of Operational Research, IEEE Transactions on Engineering Management, Computers & Industrial Engineering, Operations Research Letters, and Annals of Operations Research, among other outlets. He serves as a member of the Youth Committee of the Systems Engineering Society of China and as a board member of the Behavioral Operations and Management Division of the Chinese Society of Operations Research.

Nina Yan is currently a full professor at the Business School, Central University of Finance and Economics, China. She received her PhD in management science from Northeastern University in March 2007. Her current research interests include platform economics, supply chain finance, and operations management. She has published over 30 papers in journals such as Decision Sciences, Naval Research Logistics, Decision Support Systems, European Journal of Operational Research, International Journal of Electronic Commerce, Journal of Business Research, International Journal of Production Economics, International Journal of Production Research, and Omega.

Huigang Liang received his MS degree in computer science and his PhD degree in healthcare information systems from Auburn University. His research interests include sociobehavioral, managerial and strategic information technology issues at both individual and organizational levels in a variety of contexts. His work has appeared in MIS Quarterly, Information Systems Research, Journal of Management Information Systems, Journal of the Association for Information Systems, MIT Sloan Management Review, Communications of the ACM, Decision Support Systems, Information Systems Journal, and Journal of Strategic Information Systems, among other outlets. His research has been funded by grants from NIH, FedEx and Sloan Foundation. He has served as an associate editor for MIS Quarterly and is currently a senior editor for Journal of the Association for Information Systems and an associate editor for Information Systems Research.
